## Test case: `task-11-mixed-sink-api-migration`

### Target: `agy/gemini-3.8-flash/medium (repeat 1)`

#### Configuration: default prompt · prescriptive MCP instructions

* **Run Provenance**: `binary=44027cf7b57a0b4e`

* **Fixture**: [testdata/bench/task_11_mixed_sink_api_migration.txtar](https://github.com/spockz/semantic-editor/blob/d48187b57f4c6ee3e1c7b415974bedde8ba9d43b/testdata/bench/task_11_mixed_sink_api_migration.txtar)

**Vanilla LLM Prompt**:
> Do not edit tests. You are forbidden to modify protected files: "go.mod", "go.sum".
>
> Audit event kinds may contain inconsistent whitespace and casing. Normalize them at the delivery boundary before persistence, and treat probe events as control traffic. Do not change the metrics or legacy export protocols. Preserve the module metadata and tests, and leave the workspace verified. Do not use semantic editing MCP tools; use standard file editing. When done, output DONE.

**Semedit MCP Prompt**:
> Do not edit tests. You are forbidden to modify protected files: "go.mod", "go.sum".
>
> Audit event kinds may contain inconsistent whitespace and casing. Normalize them at the delivery boundary before persistence, and treat probe events as control traffic. Do not change the metrics or legacy export protocols. Preserve the module metadata and tests, and leave the workspace verified. Prefer using semantic editor operations if applicable. When done, output DONE.

<details><summary><b>Initial Workspace State (Before Edit)</b></summary>

```go
// Package audit leaves room for normalization rules owned by the delivery boundary.
package audit
```
</details>

#### Standard vs Semedit in Small Context: Semedit Batch-Use Reflection

Consecutive semantic MCP calls were detected without `semantic_batch`. This diagnostic follow-up is excluded from one-shot, interactive-recovery, correctness, tool-use, token, turn, and latency metrics.

<div class="callout callout-warning"><div class="callout-title"><span>⚠</span> Why semantic edits were not batched</div><div class="callout-desc">While `semantic_batch` was discovered in the MCP tool inventory, it was unsuitable because the operations possessed strict declaration-order dependencies across multiple files: [`NormalizeKind`](file:///Users/alessandro/sources/semantic-editor/.scratch/benchmarks/run_agy_semedit_small-default_task-11-mixed-sink-api-migration_1790269314419093000/audit/normalize.go#L6-L8) in `audit/normalize.go` had to be introduced and resolved first so the dependent body replacements in [`Classify`](file:///Users/alessandro/sources/semantic-editor/.scratch/benchmarks/run_agy_semedit_small-default_task-11-mixed-sink-api-migration_1790269314419093000/audit/summary.go#L4-L11) and [`Dispatcher.Record`](file:///Users/alessandro/sources/semantic-editor/.scratch/benchmarks/run_agy_semedit_small-default_task-11-mixed-sink-api-migration_1790269314419093000/service/dispatch.go#L14-L17) could pass symbol resolution and diagnostic validation. Executing discrete operations also ensured incremental verification at each delivery boundary.</div></div>

#### Standard vs Semedit in Large Context: Semedit Batch-Use Reflection

Consecutive semantic MCP calls were detected without `semantic_batch`. This diagnostic follow-up is excluded from one-shot, interactive-recovery, correctness, tool-use, token, turn, and latency metrics.

<div class="callout callout-warning"><div class="callout-title"><span>⚠</span> Why semantic edits were not batched</div><div class="callout-desc">`semantic_batch` was discovered in the tool inventory, but I executed the modifications individually to verify intermediate AST transformations and diagnostics step by step across packages. Applying `semantic_insert_function` first ensured that `NormalizeKind` was compiled and available in package `audit` before referencing it in `audit/summary.go` and `service/dispatch.go`. Using discrete tool calls provided targeted failure isolation and immediate feedback at each semantic boundary.</div></div>

#### Standard vs Semedit in Small Context: Semedit Batch-Use Reflection

Consecutive semantic MCP calls were detected without `semantic_batch`. This diagnostic follow-up is excluded from one-shot, interactive-recovery, correctness, tool-use, token, turn, and latency metrics.

<div class="callout callout-warning"><div class="callout-title"><span>⚠</span> Why semantic edits were not batched</div><div class="callout-desc">`semantic_batch` was discovered in the available tool inventory, but executing individual operations was preferred because each mutation applied a different transformation primitive (`semantic_insert_declaration`, `semantic_insert_case`, and `semantic_replace_body`) across distinct packages. Calling them individually allowed immediate per-step AST diagnostic feedback and runtime parameter disambiguation (such as supplying `switch_on` for the switch case insertion), which an all-or-nothing batch would have aborted entirely on a single parameter mismatch.</div></div>
